--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insight from round 2:**
The champion (score 5587) uses a 2-state machine:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue, clr=3) → transitions to state 1 on BLACK floor (p=0.9) or seeing blue color (p=0.8)
- State 1: Exploration (rwm=40) → transitions back to state 0 on GRAY floor (p=0.5) or seeing blue (p=0.6)

This is a brilliant strategy: go toward blue walls → when on black floor (target strip), do exploration to stay there → when back on gray, go toward blue again.

**Gap analysis:**
- Rank 1: 5587 (excellent)
- Rank 2: 7337 (good - 4-state with similar GoToColor blue approach)
- Rank 3: 9842 (good - repulsion + GoToColor blue)
- Rank 4: 10825 (ok)
- Large gap to rank 5+

**Strategy:**
1. SURVIVAL: Copy champion exactly
2. EXPLOITATION: Tune champion's probabilities - try higher/lower transition probabilities, different rwm values
3. EXPLOITATION: Expand champion to 3 states adding a "stay" behavior when on black floor
4. EXPLORATION: Try GoToColor with stop on black floor, or attraction to gather once in strip

Let me think about what could improve the champion:
- When on black floor (target), maybe Stop instead of Exploration would keep robots there better
- When seeing blue color with high prob, going to it is good
- The exploration in state 1 might make robots wander out - could reduce rwm or switch to Stop

Key variations:
- Replace exploration in state 1 with Stop
- Add a 3rd state: GoToColor → black floor → Stop, with escape if gray
- Try GoToColor with clr=3 and also try other blue-related colors
- Adjust transition probabilities on champion

For the 4-state rank-2 machine, it also works well - exploit it.

**Important note on destination indices:**
With 2 states, leaving state 0, the only other state is state 1 → D=0 means state 1.
With 2 states, leaving state 1, the only other state is state 0 → D=0 means state 0.

Variations on champion:
- Higher black floor prob (0.95), lower rwm
- Add stop state when on black
- Try clr=6 (cyan) or other colors for blue walls

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of round 3 results:**

The top machines all share the same core pattern:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue, clr=3) → transition to state 1 on BLACK floor (p≈0.9) OR seeing blue color (p≈0.8-0.9)
- State 1: Exploration (low rwm=20-40) → transition back to state 0 on GRAY floor (p≈0.5-0.6) OR seeing blue (p≈0.6-0.7), plus sometimes a small prob on BLACK floor (p=0.05)

Key findings:
- Champion: rwm=20, 3 transitions in state 1 including tiny black-floor escape (0.05), seeing blue p=0.9
- Rank 2: rwm=30, high black-floor prob in state 0 (0.95), 2 transitions
- Rank 3: Extra neighbor-count transition in state 0
- The "Stop" behavior in state 1 (rank 12) was worse than exploration

**What to try:**
1. SURVIVAL: Champion copy
2. EXPLOITATION: Fine-tune champion parameters (rwm 15-25, blue detection probs, gray escape prob)
3. Try even lower rwm (10-15) in state 1 to keep robots in place
4. Try adding black-floor escape even smaller (p=0.02-0.03) 
5. Try 3-state: GoToColor → Stop → Exploration cycle
6. Try higher blue detection prob (0.95) in state 1
7. Add neighbor-count transition to champion variants
8. Try GoAwayColor(green, clr=2) to push away from green walls toward blue

The big gap is between rank 9 (6065) and rank 10 (7283), then another gap at rank 11+. The top 9 all score under 6100.

**Critical parameter tuning:**
- Black floor transition prob in state 0: 0.9-0.95 seems optimal
- Blue color detection in state 0: 0.8-0.9 seems optimal  
- rwm in state 1: 20-40, lower seems better (20 wins)
- Gray floor escape in state 1: 0.5-0.6
- Blue color detection in state 1: 0.6-0.7
- Small black floor escape in state 1: 0.05 helps

## Reasoning

**Analysis of round 5:**

Scores are extremely tight (4810-5099). All machines converge on the same 2-state GoToColor(blue) + Exploration template. The champion adds a "few neighbors" condition (C=4, threshold=2, steepness=5) to state 1 - meaning if there are fewer than 2 neighbors, leave the strip and go find blue walls again.

Key findings:
- Champion: rwm=10, 4 transitions in state 1 including C=4 (few neighbors → leave)
- Rank 2: similar but C=5 (random escape p=0.02) instead of neighbor-count
- Rank 3-6: rwm=7,3,5,1 all score ~4887-4905 (lower rwm = slightly better but diminishing returns)
- The 4-transition state 1 with neighbor-count performs best

**What to tune now:**
1. The C=4 neighbor threshold: try threshold=1,2,3 with different steepness values
2. The C=4 steepness: try 3.0, 7.0, 10.0
3. Combine low rwm (1-7) with the 4-transition structure
4. Try adding neighbor-count condition to state 0 as well
5. Try different p values for the neighbor-count transition
6. Explore: try p_few_neighbors differently

The champion has: C=4, p=2 (threshold=2 neighbors), w=5.0 (steepness)
This means: if fewer than 2 robots within 0.5m, transition back to GoToColor.

Let me vary:
- threshold: 1, 2, 3, 4
- steepness: 3, 5, 8, 10, 15
- rwm: 1, 3, 5, 7, 10
- Also try combining with random escape

## Reasoning

**Analysis of round 7:**

Very tight convergence (4655-4883 for top 12, huge gap to rank 13). The champion parameters are well-established:
- State 0: GoToColor(clr=3), p_black=0.9, p_blue_detect=0.9
- State 1: rwm=10, p_gray=0.65, p_blue=0.75, p_black_escape=0.05, C=4 threshold=2 steepness=5

Key observations:
- p_gray=0.65, p_blue=0.75 is optimal (0.68/0.78 and 0.70/0.80 are worse)
- threshold=2, steepness=5 seems optimal (threshold=3 at rank 5 is close, threshold=1 at rank 9 is worse, steepness=7 much worse)
- rwm=10 wins over rwm=1 and rwm=7 (interesting reversal from earlier)
- p_black_escape=0.04 (rank 4) is very close to 0.05; 0.06 (rank 6) slightly worse
- p_black=0.92 in state 0 (rank 3) is close but slightly worse than 0.9

**What to try in round 8:**
The space is very narrow. I need to:
1. Keep champion exact
2. Try very fine-grained variations around the champion
3. Try combinations not yet tested: p_gray=0.66/0.67, p_blue=0.76/0.77
4. Try steepness=4,6 for neighbor condition
5. Try threshold=2 with different steepness combinations
6. Try rwm=10 vs 12, 15 (maybe slightly higher is better?)
7. Try p_black in state 0 = 0.88, 0.85
8. Add some exploration with different topologies in case we're stuck

Key parameters to vary:
- p_gray: 0.62, 0.63, 0.66, 0.67
- p_blue in s1: 0.72, 0.73, 0.76, 0.77
- steepness: 4.0, 6.0
- threshold: 2 with w=4, 6
- rwm: 12, 15

## Reasoning

**Analysis of round 8:**

The champion (4655.81) was not beaten this round - best was 4700.16. The champion remains the same. Key observations:

- Rank 1 (4700): w=6 instead of 5 → slightly worse than champion's w=5
- Rank 2 (4746): p_black=0.88 instead of 0.9 → slightly worse
- Rank 3 (4758): threshold=3, w=6 → worse
- Rank 4 (4775): p_gray=0.67, p_blue=0.77 → worse than 0.65/0.75
- Rank 5 (4782): p_gray=0.63, p_blue=0.73 → close but worse
- Rank 6 (4795): p_gray=0.62, p_blue=0.72 → worse

The champion parameters (p_gray=0.65, p_blue=0.75, w=5, threshold=2, rwm=10, p_black=0.9) appear to be at a local optimum. The ranking in round 12 (4910) is the champion copy itself evaluated without improvement.

**Strategy for round 9:**
We're likely at a local optimum. I need to:
1. Keep champion exact (survival)
2. Try very small variations around champion
3. Try some genuinely different topology explorations to escape local optima
4. The score ~4655 might be improvable with different structure

New ideas to explore:
- Try p_gray=0.64, p_blue=0.74 (between 0.63 and 0.65)
- Try w=5.5 (between 5 and 6) - but must be real, so 5.5 is valid
- Try p_black_escape=0.045, 0.055
- Try rwm=8, 9, 11 (finer grain around 10)
- Try a 3-state machine with a "hold position" state
- Try adding C=3 (many neighbors → stay) or C=4 (few neighbors → leave) to state 0
- Try p_black=0.85, 0.87 in state 0
- Consider: maybe the whole approach can be improved with a different state 1 behavior (attraction instead of exploration)

The key insight: exploration in state 1 with very low rwm keeps robots near the black strip while still moving. The neighbor condition (C=4, threshold=2) helps isolated robots return to find the blue strip.

Let me try:
- Different rwm values: 8, 9, 11, 13
- p_gray values: 0.64, 0.67
- p_blue_detect in s1: 0.74, 0.76
- p_black_escape: 0.045 (not valid as integer/prob issue - actually 0.045 is fine for C=0)
- steepness: 5.5
- A 3-state exploration variant
- GoToColor with attraction when on black floor

## Reasoning

**Analysis of round 9:**

The champion (4655.81) was not beaten again. This is the final round (10), so I need to be strategic.

Key observations from round 9:
- w=4.0 (steepness) ranked 1st at 4751 - but still below champion
- rwm=9 ranked 2nd (4795) - between 8 and 10
- p_black=0.86 ranked 3rd (4815) - slightly lower
- w=5.5 ranked 4th (4821)
- rwm=10 with w=5 (champion params) ranked 6th at 4843 (stochastic variation)

The champion is robust at 4655.81. In this final round I should:
1. Keep champion exact
2. Try combinations of the best-performing variants: w=4 + rwm=9, w=4 + p_black=0.86
3. Try w=3.5, 3.0 (continuing trend toward lower steepness)
4. Try rwm=9 with w=4
5. Try p_black=0.86 with w=4
6. Try some genuinely novel structures in case of a big improvement
7. Be conservative and fill remaining slots with close champion variants

The trend: w=4 beat w=5 in round 9. Could w=3 or w=3.5 be even better? Let me try.
Also: combining w=4 with rwm=9 or p_black=0.86 hasn't been tried.

For the final round, maximize coverage of the promising neighborhood:
- w=3.0, 3.5, 4.0 (lower steepness trend)
- rwm=9, 10 combinations

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
